  00066: rtw88_skb_tx_completion_dispatch(
  00067:     struct rtw88_skb_tx_completion_token *token, bool transmitted)
  00068: {
  00069:     if (!token)
  00070:         return false;
  00071: 
  00072:     rtw88_skb_tx_completion_fn callback = token->callback;
  00073:     rtw88_skb_tx_completion_release_fn release = token->release;
  00074:     void *context = token->context;
  00075:     void *cookie = token->cookie;
  00076:

  00078:         callback(context, cookie, transmitted);
  00079:     if (release)
  00080:         release(token);

  00075: 
  00076: IOReturn RTW88WCLSkywalkTxAdapter::submitPacket(IOSkywalkPacket *packet)
  00077: {
  00078:     if (!packet)
  00079:         return kIOReturnBadArgument;
  00080:     if (!_radio || !_txPool)
  00081:         return kIOReturnNotReady;
  00082: 
  00083:     RTW88WCLPacketView view = {};
  00084:     if (!RTW88WCLResolveEthernetTxPacket(packet, _txPool, &view))
  00085:         return kIOReturnBadArgument;
  00086: 
  00087:     mbuf_t mbuf = copyPacketToMbuf(view.bytes, view.length);
  00088:     if (!mbuf)
  00089:         return kIOReturnNoMemory;
  00090: 
  00091:     packet->retain();
  00092:     TicketPool::Ticket *ticket = _tickets.reserve(packet);
  00093:     if (!ticket) {
  00094:         packet->release();
  00095:         mbuf_freem(mbuf);
  00096:         return kIOReturnBusy;
  00097:     }
  00098: 
  00099:     /* Keep the adapter alive until the workloop-side completion drain drops
  00100:      * this ticket. backendCompletion() intentionally does not release it. */
  00101:     retain();
  00102:     const UInt32 backendResult = _radio->outputPacketWithCompletion(
  00103:         mbuf, &RTW88WCLSkywalkTxAdapter::backendCompletion, this, ticket);
  00104: 
  00105:     /* outputPacketWithCompletion consumes the mbuf and reports exactly one
  00106:      * terminal callback, including synchronous drops. Once the ticket has
  00107:      * been reserved, adapter acceptance is successful; the actual transmitted
  00108:      * vs dropped result is delivered later through the completion sink. */
  00109:     (void)backendResult;
  00110:     return kIOReturnSuccess;
  00111: }
  00112: 
  00113: void RTW88WCLSkywalkTxAdapter::backendCompletion(
  00114:     void *context, void *cookie, bool transmitted)
  00115: {
  00116:     auto *self = static_cast<RTW88WCLSkywalkTxAdapter *>(context);
  00117:     auto *ticket = static_cast<TicketPool::Ticket *>(cookie);
  00118:     if (!self || !ticket)
  00119:         return;
  00120: 
  00121:     /* No Skywalk API, OSObject release, allocation, or blocking operation is
  00122:      * allowed here. A deferred consumer performs the completion handoff. */
  00123:     (void)TicketPool::complete(ticket, transmitted);
  00124: }
  00125: 
  00126: uint32_t RTW88WCLSkywalkTxAdapter::drainCompletions(
  00127:     CompletionSink sink, void *context)
  00128: {
  00129:     if (!sink)
  00130:         return 0;
  00131: 
  00132:     /* A sink may trigger owner teardown; keep this adapter alive until the
  00133:      * drain loop has stopped touching its ticket pool. */
  00134:     retain();
  00135:     uint32_t drained = 0;
  00136:     for (;;) {
  00137:         TicketPool::Ticket *ticket = _tickets.claimCompleted();
  00138:         if (!ticket)
  00139:             break;
  00140: 
  00141:         auto *packet = static_cast<IOSkywalkPacket *>(TicketPool::packet(ticket));
  00142:         const bool transmitted = TicketPool::transmitted(ticket);
  00143: 
  00144:         /* The sink runs in deferred context and borrows the retained packet.
  00145:          * It must finish any Skywalk queue handoff before returning. */
  00146:         if (packet)
  00147:             sink(packet, transmitted, context);
  00148: 
  00149:         TicketPool::finish(ticket);
  00150:         if (packet)
  00151:             packet->release();
  00152:         release();
  00153:         ++drained;
  00154:     }
  00155:     release();
  00156:     return drained;
  00157: }

  00009:

  00011: 
  00012: /* Freestanding so the state machine can be stress-tested outside the kernel. */
  00013: template <uint32_t Capacity>
  00014: class RTW88WCLTxCompletionSlots {
  00015:     static_assert(Capacity > 0, "completion slot capacity must be non-zero");
  00016: 
  00017: public:
  00018:     enum : uint32_t {
  00019:         kFree = 0,
  00020:         kReserved,
  00021:         kSubmitted,
  00022:         kCompleting,
CONTEXT: driver/RTL88WiFi/src/kext/wcl24h32/RTW88WCLTxCompletionSlots.hpp:77-92
  00077:             return false;
  00078:         ticket->transmitted = transmitted ? 1U : 0U;
  00079:         __atomic_store_n(&ticket->state, kCompleted, __ATOMIC_RELEASE);
  00080:         return true;
  00081:     }
  00082: 
  00083:     /* Single consumer, called from the adapter's deferred/workloop context. */
  00084:     Ticket *claimCompleted()
  00085:     {
  00086:         for (uint32_t i = 0; i < Capacity; ++i) {
  00087:             Ticket *ticket = &_slots[i];
  00088:             uint32_t expected = kCompleted;
  00089:             if (__atomic_compare_exchange_n(&ticket->state, &expected,
  00090:                                             kDraining, false,
  00091:                                             __ATOMIC_ACQ_REL,
  00092:                                             __ATOMIC_ACQUIRE))

  01853:      * regression aggregation otherwise causes. */

  00024:                                   dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag);

  00036:

  00039: static inline void *dma_alloc_coherent(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00040:                                         dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag)
  00041: {
  00042:     if (rtw88_dma_ops && rtw88_dma_ops->alloc_coherent)
  00043:         return rtw88_dma_ops->alloc_coherent(dev, size, dma_handle, flag);
  00044:     /* Fallback: plain kernel allocation (not DMA-safe) */
  00045:     void *ptr = kzalloc(size, GFP_KERNEL);
  00046:     if (ptr) *dma_handle = (dma_addr_t)(uintptr_t)ptr;
  00047:     return ptr;
  00048: }
  00049: 
  00050: static inline void dma_free_coherent(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00051:                                       void *cpu_addr, dma_addr_t dma_handle)
  00052: {
  00053:     if (rtw88_dma_ops && rtw88_dma_ops->free_coherent) {
  00054:         rtw88_dma_ops->free_coherent(dev, size, cpu_addr, dma_handle);
  00055:         return;
  00056:     }
  00057:     kfree(cpu_addr);
  00058: }
  00059:

  00095: /* Match what compat_dma_map actually returns on failure: it returns 0 when
  00096:  * the bounce-buffer alloc fails.  Treat BOTH 0 and -1 as errors so the
  00097:  * driver doesn't silently program HW descriptors with bogus DMA addresses
  00098:  * (which corrupts the TX ring state — wp advances, HW chews garbage and
  00099:  * advances rp, eventually deadlocking at wp+1 == rp). */
  00100: static inline bool dma_mapping_error(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr)
  00101: {
  00102:     return addr == 0 || addr == (dma_addr_t)-1;
  00103: }
  00104: 
  00105: /* Page abstraction for RX ring allocations */
  00106: struct page {
  00107:     void      *virt;
  00108:     dma_addr_t phys;
  00109: };
  00110: 
  00111: static inline struct page *alloc_page(gfp_t flags)
  00112: {
  00113:     /* Allocate struct page + 4096-byte data in one chunk */
  00114:     struct page *p = (struct page *)kzalloc(sizeof(*p) + 4096, flags);
  00115:     if (p) {
  00116:         p->virt = (void *)((char *)p + sizeof(*p));
  00117:         p->phys = (dma_addr_t)(uintptr_t)p->virt;
  00118:     }
  00119:     return p;
  00120: }
  00121: 
  00122: static inline void __free_page(struct page *page)
  00123: {
  00124:     kfree(page);
  00125: }
  00126: 
  00127: static inline void *page_address(struct page *page)
  00128: {

  00130: }

  00108:                                dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag)
  00109: {
  00110:     if (!g_pci_dev_instance) return nullptr;
  00111:     IOPhysicalAddress phys = 0;
  00112:     void *virt = g_pci_dev_instance->allocCoherent(size, &phys);
  00113:     if (dma_handle) *dma_handle = (dma_addr_t)phys;
  00114:     return virt;
  00115: }
  00116: static void compat_dma_free(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00117:                              void *cpu_addr, dma_addr_t dma_handle)
  00118: {
  00119:     if (g_pci_dev_instance)
  00120:         g_pci_dev_instance->freeCoherent(size, cpu_addr,
  00121:                                           (IOPhysicalAddress)dma_handle);
  00122: }
  00123: static dma_addr_t compat_dma_map(struct device *dev, void *ptr,
  00124:                                    size_t size, int dir)
  00125: {
  00126:     /*
  00127:      * Always bounce: allocate a physically-contiguous <4GB buffer, record the
  00128:      * original CPU VA in orig_va, and hand the chip the bounce PA.
  00129:      *
  00130:      * DMA_TO_DEVICE (1): copy ptr→bounce now; chip reads from bounce.
  00131:      * DMA_FROM_DEVICE (0): don't copy now; chip writes to bounce.
CONTEXT: driver/RTL88WiFi/src/kext/RTW88PCIDevice.cpp:134-190
  00134:      * This is the standard software bounce-buffer strategy.  It costs a memcpy
  00135:      * per RX packet but is always correct regardless of where IOMalloc places
  00136:      * the skb data pages.
  00137:      */
  00138:     if (!g_pci_dev_instance || !ptr) return 0;
  00139: 
  00140:     IOPhysicalAddress phys = 0;
  00141:     void *bounce = g_pci_dev_instance->allocCoherent(size, &phys);
  00142:     if (!bounce) return 0;
  00143: 
  00144:     /* Store the original VA so sync_for_cpu can copy the received data back */
  00145:     g_pci_dev_instance->setBounceOrigVA((IOPhysicalAddress)phys, ptr);
  00146: 
  00147:     /* TX: feed the chip data now */
  00148:     if (dir == 1 /* DMA_TO_DEVICE */ || dir == 2 /* DMA_BIDIRECTIONAL */)
  00149:         memcpy(bounce, ptr, size);
  00150: 
  00151:     return (dma_addr_t)phys;
  00152: }
  00153: static void compat_dma_unmap(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00154:                                size_t size, int dir)
  00155: {
  00156:     /* Free the bounce buffer; freeCoherentByPhys is a no-op if not found */
  00157:     if (g_pci_dev_instance)
  00158:         g_pci_dev_instance->freeCoherentByPhys((IOPhysicalAddress)addr);
  00159: }
  00160: static void compat_dma_sync_cpu(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00161:                                   size_t size, int dir)
  00162: {
  00163:     /*
  00164:      * RX: chip finished writing to the bounce buffer.  Copy it into the
  00165:      * original skb->data buffer so the driver can read it from there.
  00166:      */
  00167:     if (dir == 0 /* DMA_FROM_DEVICE */ && g_pci_dev_instance)
  00168:         g_pci_dev_instance->syncBounceForCpu((IOPhysicalAddress)addr, size);
  00169: }
  00170: static void compat_dma_sync_dev(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00171:                                   size_t size, int dir)
  00172: {
  00173:     /* Re-arm for next DMA: bounce is already in place, nothing to do */
  00174: }

  00019:  * The C driver code calls these helpers; the kext installs rtw88_dma_ops
  00020:  * before probe so the fallback path never runs in production.
  00021:  */
  00022: struct rtw88_dma_alloc_ops {
  00023:     void      *(*alloc_coherent)(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00024:                                   dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag);
  00025:     void       (*free_coherent)(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00026:                                  void *cpu_addr, dma_addr_t dma_handle);
  00027:     dma_addr_t (*map_single)(struct device *dev, void *ptr,
  00028:                               size_t size, int direction);
  00029:     void       (*unmap_single)(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00030:                                 size_t size, int direction);
  00031:     void       (*sync_single_for_cpu)(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00032:                                        size_t size, int dir);
  00033:     void       (*sync_single_for_device)(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00034:                                           size_t size, int dir);
  00035: };
  00036: 
  00037: extern struct rtw88_dma_alloc_ops *rtw88_dma_ops;
  00038: 
  00039: static inline void *dma_alloc_coherent(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00040:                                         dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag)
  00041: {
  00042:     if (rtw88_dma_ops && rtw88_dma_ops->alloc_coherent)
  00043:         return rtw88_dma_ops->alloc_coherent(dev, size, dma_handle, flag);
  00044:     /* Fallback: plain kernel allocation (not DMA-safe) */
  00045:     void *ptr = kzalloc(size, GFP_KERNEL);
  00046:     if (ptr) *dma_handle = (dma_addr_t)(uintptr_t)ptr;
  00047:     return ptr;
  00048: }
  00049: 
  00050: static inline void dma_free_coherent(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00051:                                       void *cpu_addr, dma_addr_t dma_handle)
  00052: {
  00053:     if (rtw88_dma_ops && rtw88_dma_ops->free_coherent) {
  00054:         rtw88_dma_ops->free_coherent(dev, size, cpu_addr, dma_handle);
  00055:         return;
  00056:     }
  00057:     kfree(cpu_addr);
  00058: }

  00061:                                           size_t size, int direction)
  00062: {

  00088:         rtw88_dma_ops->sync_single_for_device(dev, addr, size, dir);
  00089: }

  00096:  * the bounce-buffer alloc fails.  Treat BOTH 0 and -1 as errors so the
  00097:  * driver doesn't silently program HW descriptors with bogus DMA addresses
  00098:  * (which corrupts the TX ring state — wp advances, HW chews garbage and
  00099:  * advances rp, eventually deadlocking at wp+1 == rp). */

  00546: void *RTL88WiFi::allocCoherent(size_t size, IOPhysicalAddress *phys)
  00547: {
  00548:     /* DMA unmaps can be deferred by IRQ/NAPI. Reclaim them whenever allocation
  00549:      * returns to a sleepable thread so sustained traffic cannot grow the list. */
  00550:     if (preemption_enabled())
  00551:         drainPendingFree();
  00552: 
  00553:     IOBufferMemoryDescriptor *desc = IOBufferMemoryDescriptor::inTaskWithPhysicalMask(
  00554:         kernel_task,
  00555:         kIOMemoryPhysicallyContiguous | kIODirectionInOut | kIOMemoryKernelUserShared,
  00556:         size,
  00557:         0x00000000FFFFFFF0ULL);
  00558:     if (!desc) { IOLog("RTL88WiFi: dma alloc failed, size=%zu\n", size); return nullptr; }
  00559:     if (desc->prepare() != kIOReturnSuccess) { desc->release(); return nullptr; }
  00560: 
  00561:     IOPhysicalAddress pa = desc->getPhysicalAddress();
  00562:     void *va = desc->getBytesNoCopy();
  00563:     if (!va || !pa) { desc->complete(); desc->release(); return nullptr; }
  00564:     memset(va, 0, size);
  00565:     if (phys) *phys = pa;
  00566: 
  00567:     RTW88DMAEntry *entry = (RTW88DMAEntry *)IOMallocZero(sizeof(RTW88DMAEntry));
  00568:     if (!entry) { desc->complete(); desc->release(); return nullptr; }
  00569:     entry->desc = desc; entry->virt = va; entry->phys = pa; entry->size = size;
  00570: 
  00571:     IOSimpleLockLock(_dmaLock);
  00572:     entry->next = _dmaList;
  00573:     _dmaList = entry;
  00574:     IOSimpleLockUnlock(_dmaLock);
  00575:     return va;
  00576: }

  00578: void RTL88WiFi::freeCoherent(size_t size, void *virt, IOPhysicalAddress phys)
  00579: {
  00580:     IOSimpleLockLock(_dmaLock);
  00581:     RTW88DMAEntry **prev = &_dmaList;
  00582:     for (RTW88DMAEntry *e = _dmaList; e; e = e->next) {
  00583:         if (e->virt == virt) {
  00584:             *prev = e->next;
  00585:             IOSimpleLockUnlock(_dmaLock);
  00586:             if (preemption_enabled()) {
  00587:                 e->desc->complete(); e->desc->release(); IOFree(e, sizeof(*e));
  00588:             } else {
  00589:                 IOSimpleLockLock(_pendingFreeLock);
  00590:                 e->next = _dmaPendingFree; _dmaPendingFree = e;
  00591:                 IOSimpleLockUnlock(_pendingFreeLock);
  00592:             }
  00593:             return;
  00594:         }
  00595:         prev = &e->next;
  00596:     }
  00597:     IOSimpleLockUnlock(_dmaLock);
  00598:     IOLog("RTL88WiFi: freeCoherent: virt %p not found\n", virt);
  00599: }

  00101: static dma_addr_t compat_dma_map(struct device *, void *ptr, size_t size, int dir)
  00102: {
  00103:     if (!g_pci_dev_instance || !ptr) return 0;
  00104:

  00113: }

  00107: static void *compat_dma_alloc(struct device *dev, size_t size,
  00108:                                dma_addr_t *dma_handle, gfp_t flag)
  00109: {
  00110:     if (!g_pci_dev_instance) return nullptr;
  00111:     IOPhysicalAddress phys = 0;
  00112:     void *virt = g_pci_dev_instance->allocCoherent(size, &phys);
  00113:     if (dma_handle) *dma_handle = (dma_addr_t)phys;

  00117:                              void *cpu_addr, dma_addr_t dma_handle)

  00121:                                           (IOPhysicalAddress)dma_handle);
  00122: }
  00123: static dma_addr_t compat_dma_map(struct device *dev, void *ptr,
  00124:                                    size_t size, int dir)
  00125: {
  00126:     /*
  00127:      * Always bounce: allocate a physically-contiguous <4GB buffer, record the
  00128:      * original CPU VA in orig_va, and hand the chip the bounce PA.
  00129:      *
CONTEXT: driver/RTL88WiFi/src/kext/RTW88PCIDevice.cpp:133-186
  00133:      *
  00134:      * This is the standard software bounce-buffer strategy.  It costs a memcpy
  00135:      * per RX packet but is always correct regardless of where IOMalloc places
  00136:      * the skb data pages.
  00137:      */
  00138:     if (!g_pci_dev_instance || !ptr) return 0;
  00139: 
  00140:     IOPhysicalAddress phys = 0;
  00141:     void *bounce = g_pci_dev_instance->allocCoherent(size, &phys);
  00142:     if (!bounce) return 0;
  00143: 
  00144:     /* Store the original VA so sync_for_cpu can copy the received data back */
  00145:     g_pci_dev_instance->setBounceOrigVA((IOPhysicalAddress)phys, ptr);
  00146: 
  00147:     /* TX: feed the chip data now */
  00148:     if (dir == 1 /* DMA_TO_DEVICE */ || dir == 2 /* DMA_BIDIRECTIONAL */)
  00149:         memcpy(bounce, ptr, size);
  00150: 
  00151:     return (dma_addr_t)phys;
  00152: }
  00153: static void compat_dma_unmap(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00154:                                size_t size, int dir)
  00155: {
  00156:     /* Free the bounce buffer; freeCoherentByPhys is a no-op if not found */
  00157:     if (g_pci_dev_instance)
  00158:         g_pci_dev_instance->freeCoherentByPhys((IOPhysicalAddress)addr);
  00159: }
  00160: static void compat_dma_sync_cpu(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00161:                                   size_t size, int dir)
  00162: {
  00163:     /*
  00164:      * RX: chip finished writing to the bounce buffer.  Copy it into the
  00165:      * original skb->data buffer so the driver can read it from there.
  00166:      */
  00167:     if (dir == 0 /* DMA_FROM_DEVICE */ && g_pci_dev_instance)
  00168:         g_pci_dev_instance->syncBounceForCpu((IOPhysicalAddress)addr, size);
  00169: }
  00170: static void compat_dma_sync_dev(struct device *dev, dma_addr_t addr,
  00171:                                   size_t size, int dir)
  00172: {
  00173:     /* Re-arm for next DMA: bounce is already in place, nothing to do */
  00174: }

  00750: void *RTW88PCIDevice::allocCoherent(size_t size, IOPhysicalAddress *phys)
  00751: {
  00752:     /*
  00753:      * rtw88 TX ring descriptors store DMA addresses in 32-bit fields.
  00754:      * Restrict physical allocation to the first 4GB so truncation to
  00755:      * cpu_to_le32() in the ring descriptor is lossless.
  00756:      * 0x00000000FFFFFFF0 = below 4GB, 16-byte aligned.
  00757:      */
  00758:     IOBufferMemoryDescriptor *desc = IOBufferMemoryDescriptor::inTaskWithPhysicalMask(
  00759:         kernel_task,
  00760:         kIOMemoryPhysicallyContiguous | kIODirectionInOut | kIOMemoryKernelUserShared,
  00761:         size,
  00762:         0x00000000FFFFFFF0ULL);
  00763: 
  00764:     if (!desc) { IOLog("rtw88: dma alloc failed, size=%zu\n", size); return nullptr; }
  00765:     desc->prepare();
  00766: 
  00767:     IOPhysicalAddress pa = desc->getPhysicalAddress();
  00768:     void *va = desc->getBytesNoCopy();
  00769:     memset(va, 0, size);
  00770: 
  00771:     if (phys) *phys = pa;
  00772: 
  00773:     /* Allocate the tracking node OUTSIDE the spinlock — IOMallocZero is
  00774:      * sleepable, and IOSimpleLock disables preemption.  Calling a sleepable
  00775:      * allocator from a preemption-disabled context panics XNU with
  00776:      * "blocking while holding a spinlock". */
  00777:     DMAEntry *entry = (DMAEntry *)IOMallocZero(sizeof(DMAEntry));
  00778:     if (!entry) {
  00779:         desc->complete();
  00780:         desc->release();
  00781:         return nullptr;
  00782:     }
  00783:     entry->desc = desc;
  00784:     entry->virt = va;
  00785:     entry->phys = pa;
  00786:     entry->size = size;
  00787: 
CONTEXT: driver/RTL88WiFi/src/kext/RTW88PCIDevice.cpp:789-839
  00789:     entry->next = _dmaList;
  00790:     _dmaList = entry;
  00791:     IOSimpleLockUnlock(_dmaLock);
  00792: 
  00793:     return va;
  00794: }

  00796: void RTW88PCIDevice::freeCoherent(size_t size, void *virt, IOPhysicalAddress phys)
  00797: {
  00798:     IOSimpleLockLock(_dmaLock);
  00799:     DMAEntry **prev = &_dmaList;

  00801:         if (e->virt == virt) {
  00802:             *prev = e->next;
  00803:             IOSimpleLockUnlock(_dmaLock);
  00804:             if (preemption_enabled()) {
  00805:                 e->desc->complete();
  00806:                 e->desc->release();

  00812:                 IOSimpleLockUnlock(_pendingFreeLock);

  00817:     }

  00364: void flush_work(struct work_struct *work)
  00365: {
  00366:     if (!work || !work_state_lock) return;
  00367:     IOLockLock(work_state_lock);
  00368:     while (work->pending || work->executing)
  00369:         IOLockSleep(work_state_lock, work, THREAD_UNINT);
  00370:     IOLockUnlock(work_state_lock);
  00371: }

  00192: IOReturn RTW88WCLController::releaseNativeResources()
  00193: {
  00194:     const IOReturn packetPathStatus = quiescePacketPath();
  00195:     if (packetPathStatus != kIOReturnSuccess) {
  00196:         IOLog("rtl88wifi-wcl: packet path quiesce deferred: 0x%08x\n",
  00197:               packetPathStatus);
  00198:         return packetPathStatus; // Keep the radio, interface and PCI callbacks alive.
  00199:     }
  00200: 
  00201:     /* Stop the RTW88 RX/TX producer before removing its deferred consumers or
  00202:      * disabling PCI interrupts. The staged path currently leaves
  00203:      * _radioStarted false; preserve this ordering when native radio start is
  00204:      * enabled after queue registration. */
  00205:     if (_radio && _radioStarted) {
  00206:         _radio->stop();
  00207:         _radioStarted = false;
  00208:     }
  00209: 
  00210:     if (_pciBackend && _pciBackend->isRadioRunning()) {
  00211:         const IOReturn fenceStatus = _pciBackend->setRadioRunning(false);
  00212:         if (fenceStatus != kIOReturnSuccess) {
  00213:             IOLog("rtl88wifi-wcl: PCI interrupt/bus-master fence failed: 0x%08x\n",
  00214:                   fenceStatus);
  00215:             return fenceStatus; // Keep callbacks, mappings and DMA owner alive on failure.
  00216:         }
  00217:     }
  00218: 
  00219:     /* Remove the RX workloop source only after the RTW88 producer is stopped.
  00220:      * stop() waits out an active checkForWork() and releases all staged mbufs. */
  00221:     if (_rxAdapter) {
  00222:         _rxAdapter->stop();
  00223:         _rxAdapter->release();
  00224:         _rxAdapter = nullptr;
  00225:     }
  00226: 
  00227:     /* The RX adapter transfers accepted native packets into the controller's
  00228:      * bounded ready ring. Recycle those packets while their pool still exists;
  00229:      * on a pool error, retain the queue owner and PCI/backend context so a later
  00230:      * stop/free attempt can retry rather than releasing live packet memory. */

  00428: IOReturn RTW88WCLController::quiescePacketPath()
  00429: {
  00430:     for (;;) {
  00431:         uint32_t state = __atomic_load_n(&_packetPathState, __ATOMIC_ACQUIRE);
  00432:         if (state == kPacketPathDisabled)
  00433:             return kIOReturnSuccess;
  00434:         if (state == kPacketPathBinding)
  00435:             return kIOReturnBusy;
  00436:         if (state == kPacketPathActive) {
  00437:             uint32_t expected = kPacketPathActive;
  00438:             if (!__atomic_compare_exchange_n(&_packetPathState, &expected,
  00439:                                              kPacketPathQuiescing, false,
  00440:                                              __ATOMIC_ACQ_REL, __ATOMIC_ACQUIRE))
  00441:                 continue;
  00442:         } else if (state != kPacketPathQuiescing) {
  00443:             return kIOReturnBusy;
  00444:         }
  00445:         break;
  00446:     }
  00447: 
  00448:     if (__atomic_load_n(&_packetPathInFlight, __ATOMIC_ACQUIRE) != 0)
  00449:         return kIOReturnBusy;
  00450: 
  00451:     uint32_t expected = kPacketPathQuiescing;
  00452:     if (!__atomic_compare_exchange_n(&_packetPathState, &expected,
  00453:                                      kPacketPathReleasing, false,
  00454:                                      __ATOMIC_ACQ_REL, __ATOMIC_ACQUIRE))
  00455:         return kIOReturnBusy;
  00456: 
  00457:     IO80211SkywalkInterface *interface = _packetPathInterface;
  00458:     _packetPathInterface = nullptr;
  00459:     if (interface)

=== STAGE25 REVIEW GATES ===
1. Identify exactly one outputPacketWithCompletion definition (or report all definitions); follow every return branch and ownership transfer.
2. Enumerate every call site and callback invocation, including synchronous failure, asynchronous completion, cancellation, reset and stop.
3. Trace ticket allocation, publication, callback lookup, completion, release and reuse; identify locking/atomic ordering.
4. Trace all RTW88 TX completion producers and prove exactly-once terminal completion for every submitted skb.
5. Pair every DMA map/alloc/prepare with unmap/free/complete across normal TX, errors, reset, failed start and stop.
6. Establish workqueue, tasklet, interrupt and DMA quiescence before reclaiming descriptors or packet memory.
7. Compare ownership transitions with existing 24H32 Skywalk adapter contracts.

STAGE25_STATUS: REVIEW_REQUIRED — source scan is evidence collection, not a semantic pass.
ACTIVATION_GATE: BLOCKED — no queue registration/enable, packet handoff, BSD publication, radio start, build/install/load or OpenCore changes.
